アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大 
供給制約への対応力が投資の持続性を左右

○ アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大している。その背景には、AI・クラウド需要の拡大や各国・
地域の投資誘致策、クラウド事業者の戦略、建設コストの低さなどが挙げられる 
○ データセンター投資が増加することは、総固定資本形成の増加や、半導体・サーバーなどの輸出
拡大、雇用創出、技術・知識の移転につながる可能性がある 
○ 今後も需要拡大が見込まれる一方、立地地域が抱える電力・水などのインフラ負荷は大きい。 
投資の経済効果と負荷とのバランスを踏まえた政策対応が、投資拡大の持続性を左右するだろう 
１．アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大 
アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大している。図表1は、同地域におけるデータセンター関連の対内
直接投資計画額を示したものである。データセンター投資は2024年頃から大きく増加し、2026年上半
期も高水準を維持している。主要な投資先であるASEAN向け投資が一段と拡大したほか、2025年以降は
インド、オーストラリアでも投資が急増している。 
また、アジアのFDI流入におけるデータセンターの存在感も高まっている。同地域の対内直接投資計
画額全体に占めるデータセンターの割合は、2025年に約30％まで上昇した（図表2）。

２．アジアにおいてデータセンター投資がなぜ増加しているのか 
アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大している背景には、投資受入国におけるデジタル関連需要の増
加や、それを受けた投資誘致の活発化、また投資側企業にとってコスト面などから当該地域の相対的
な優位性が増していることが挙げられる。 
実際、投資受入国ではデジタルサービスに対する需要が拡大している。Gelvanovska-Garcia et al.
（2024）によれば、東アジア・太平洋地域では、インターネットの普及や経済のデジタル化を背景に、
製造業や医療など幅広い分野でクラウドサービスの導入が進んでいると指摘している。こうしたサー
ビスの利用拡大は、データの処理・保存を担うデータセンターの需要を高めることにつながる。 
また、各国政府がデータセンター誘致を活発化させていることも、アジアにおけるデータセンター
投資を後押ししている。図表3は、各国のデータセンター関連の優遇策を示したものだが、各国で誘致
を目的とした優遇政策がとられている。タイやインドでは、一定の条件を満たすデータセンター事業
や関連事業者に対して、所得税等の免税措置にまで踏み込んでいる。 
対して、投資側企業にとっては、需要地の近くにデータセンターを整備することによる、自社サー
ビスの品質向上が期待される1。事実、アジアにおける主要なデータセンター投資案件の企業発表にお
いても、アジアへの投資目的として、現地企業や政府への低遅延サービス提供、通信環境の強靭化な
どが挙げられている2。 
サービス品質向上に加え、ASEANやインド等における建設コストの低さも投資を後押しする要因とな
っている。図表4は、データセンターにおけるIT負荷容量1メガワットあたりの建設コストを比較した
ものだが、インドやベトナム、インドネシアなどはデータセンターの建設費用が相対的に小さく、シ
ンガポール等の地域に比べ、低コストでデータセンターの容量増強が可能である。

図表 3 データセンター誘致策の例 図表 4 データセンターの建設コスト

３．データセンター投資はアジア経済に何をもたらすのか 
データセンター投資の拡大は、アジア経済にどのような影響をもたらすのだろうか。主なポイント
は以下の4つである。 
まず第一に、総固定資本形成の押し上げである。データセンターの設置時、企業は建物を建設する
ほか、サーバーや受変電、冷却、通信等の設備を購入する。こうした企業活動は、GDP統計上では総固
定資本形成中の建設・機械設備投資として計上され、経済成長率の押し上げ要因となる3。実際、オー
ストラリアでは2025年以降、情報・通信部門の機械投資がGDP成長率を押し上げており、データセンタ
ー投資拡大の影響がうかがえる。 
また第二のポイントは、AI関連財の輸出拡大である。東アジアやASEANは、半導体やサーバー等の製
造拠点であり、製品需要の拡大は輸出の追い風となる。図表5に示す通り、アジアのAI関連財輸出はAI
ブームのもとで急拡大が続いている。とりわけ、2026年以降は中国などアジア域内向け輸出の増勢が
顕著であり、アジア域内におけるデータセンター設置拡大の影響がみてとれる。 
そして第三のポイントは、雇用の拡大である。データセンターの設置時に建設や設備設置の作業員
が必要となるほか、稼働後も設備管理やサーバー運用に関わる人材の雇用が創出される可能性がある。 
最後は、技術・知識の波及である。データセンターを運営する外国企業が現地企業と取引すること
により、外国企業から現地企業へ技術が伝わることが期待される。例えば、電力・冷却・通信設備の
設計や施工、保守管理にかかるノウハウを、現地企業が獲得するケースなどがある。 
ただし、経済波及効果の実現には不確実性が伴う点は留意する必要がある。中でも、雇用への波及
効果は限定的なものに留まる可能性がある。というのも、fDi Marketsが集計する投資計画データによ
ると（図表6）、海外直接投資100万米ドルあたりの直接雇用創出数は、データセンター投資の場合、全
産業平均よりも少数にとどまるからだ。 
図表 5 アジアのAI関連財輸出額（仕向け地別） 図表 6 海外直接投資100万米ドルあたりの 
直接雇用創出数（2022～2025年平均）

４．データセンター投資の先行きとリスク要因 
今後のデータセンター投資は、需要面での拡大余地が大きいと見込まれる一方、供給面では様々な
制約が表面化する可能性がある。 
まず、需要面については、クラウド需要の拡大がデータセンターの投資を促す公算が大きい。実際、
主要な民間調査会社の予想によれば、電子商取引やAI利活用の浸透によりデータの保存・処理需要が
増加し、アジアにおけるクラウド市場の拡大が続くとされている4。 
一方、先行きの供給制約となりうる要因も少なくない。具体的には、電力・水などの資源制約と、
政策変更の2つである。 
資源制約については、第一に電力供給の制約がある。アジアでは、今後も電力需要の拡大が見込ま
れる一方で、それに見合った電力供給の拡大が容易ではないからだ。図表7は、IEAが2026年2月に公表
した、2026～2030年の年平均増加率の予測を示している。掲載した各国・地域ではいずれも電力需要
の増加が見込まれ、特にインドやベトナムなどで高い伸びとなっていることがわかる。産業活動の拡
大や冷房需要の増加、データセンターの集積などが需要拡大をけん引する格好だ。 
一方、電力供給の伸びは、需要と比べ、緩やかなものにとどまる可能性が高い。IEAによれば、東南
アジアの電力生産量は2026～2030年に年平均5.2％の増加と、電力需要の伸び（年平均5.4％）を下回
り、電力需給のひっ迫が続くことが想定されている5。 
また、データセンターの立地が特定地域に集中しやすい点にも留意が必要だ6。特定地域で電力需給
がひっ迫した場合、送電網の不足などで他地域からの融通ができない場合がある7。 
資源制約については、電力のみならず、水の安定供給という課題もある。冷却に大量の水を使用す
るデータセンターでは、施設の新設・拡張に伴って水需要が増加すると見込まれるからだ。必要な水
量を確保できなければ、施設の稼働が制約されることになりかねない。ASEAN事務局（2025）は、デー
タセンターの冷却に伴う水需要の増加は、農業・工業・住民生活向けの水利用と競合する可能性があ
ると指摘している8。 
二点目は、政策変更のリスクである。雇用拡大等
を通じた地場経済への貢献度の低さや、資源利用や
安全性をめぐる地域住民からの反発などを背景に、
投資許認可の厳格化や新規開発の制限が行われる
可能性がある。 
地場経済への貢献については、先述の通り、デー
タセンターの直接的な雇用創出効果は他の投資に
比べて限られる。その結果、受入国・地域が期待し
ていた経済効果よりも現地経済への貢献が小さい
となった場合、投資誘致や優遇税制の見直しにつな
がる可能性は否定できない。また、地域住民からの
反発については、図表8で示す通り、既にマレーシ
ア、インドネシア、インド、オーストラリアなどで、
水利用や生活環境への影響などを巡り、データセン

ター投資への抗議や反対運動が起きている。 
こうした状況のもと、一部の国ではすでにデータセンター投資関連の規制を見直す動きが広がって
いる。実際にタイでは、政府による委員会組織（データセンタービジネス委員会）が、データセンタ
ー166件の建設や許可手続きを一時停止した。停止の理由としては、資源の利用、立地、安全性に関す
る全国的な基準の策定が必要であることが挙げられている9。タイ政府は投資誘致を維持すると示して
いるものの、予見可能性の低下から、進捗中案件の見直しや計画中案件の中止につながるリスクは完
全に拭えない。

５．供給制約への対応力がデータセンター投資の行方を左右 
アジアでは、AI・クラウド需要の拡大を背景に、今後もデータセンター投資の増加が続くと見込ま
れる。ただし、巨額の投資に比べて雇用創出効果は限定的とみられる一方、電力・水などのインフラ
負荷は大きく、投資の拡大が必ずしも受入国・地域に十分な経済的便益をもたらすとは限らない。 
こうした中、今後のデータセンター投資では、建設コストの低さや税制優遇に加え、電力・水の安
定供給や規制・許認可の予見可能性などが、投資先を選別する上で一段と重要になると考えられる。
供給制約が深刻化する地域では、投資計画の遅延や縮小が生じる一方、安定的な事業環境を確保でき
る地域に投資が集中する可能性もある。 
受入国にとっても、単に投資額の拡大を追求するのではなく、国内産業への波及やインフラ整備の
負担を見極めることが重要となる。今後のアジアにおけるデータセンター投資は、需要拡大が成長を
支える一方で、供給制約への対応力の違いが、各国・地域のへの投資配分を左右することになるだろ
う。

図表 8 データセンターを巡る抗議活動の例

(出所）各種報道より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

インドネシア バタム島 水不足の深刻化を理由に、データセンター建設予定地へ住民 が集まり、道路を封鎖
インド ムンバイ 住民との事前協議が不足していたとして、米国大手IT企業への データセンター建設計画に対し、数百人の住民が抗議

1 JLL（2020）「データセンター投資の基礎知識」等によれば、データセンターと利用者間の物理的な距離を短縮すると、通信遅
延が減少し、データ送受信にかかる時間が短期化すると指摘されている。 
2 fDi Markets や生成 AI による情報収集により、アジアにおける主要なデータセンター投資案件に関する企業発表の内容を確認
した。マレーシア、台湾等の投資案件において、通信速度に関する内容がみられた。 
3 サーバーや関連設備を海外から輸入する場合、輸入増加が GDP を押し下げる方向に働く部分もあるため、投資額の全てが国内
付加価値の増加につながるわけではない。 
4 CBRE、McKinsey & Company 等の民間調査会社のレポートを参照した。 
5IEA（2026）“ASEAN Energy Security Review”では、東南アジアの電力需要は 2026～2030 年に年平均 5.4％増加すると予測さ
れている。電力生産量の伸びは、IEA（2026）“Electricity 2026”、 IEA（2026）“ASEAN Energy Security Review”に基づき、
IEA の 2024 年実績値と 2025 年の前年比伸び率、2030 年の予測値を基準に、年平均成長率が一定となるよう補間し計算したも
の。 
6 IEA（2025）“Energy and AI”は、データセンターは電力負荷が大きく、地理的にも集中する傾向があるため、発電能力や送
電網容量を大幅に増強する必要が生じる可能性があること、また電力供給が途切れないよう安定的な電力網を構築する必要があ
ることを指摘している。 
7 ベトナム北部では、2023 年 5～6 月に断続的な停電が発生し、多くの家庭や企業に影響が広がった。World Bank（2023）
“Taking Stock, August 2023: Making Public Investment Work for Growth”は、送電網整備の遅れにより、南部の余剰発電
能力を北部の電力供給に十分に活用できなかったことが停電の一因となったと指摘している。 
8 ASEAN（2025）“ASEAN Guide for Sustainable Data Centre Development”によれば、データセンターの中でもハイパースケ
ール施設の冷却システムでは、水使用量が 1 日当たり最大 150 万リットルに達する場合があり、干ばつが発生しやすい地域の水
供給や生態系に大きな負荷を与えうると説明している。また、水利用の効率化を促す政策的枠組みがなければ、データセンター
の水需要が、農業・工業・地域住民に必要な水の確保を圧迫する可能性を指摘している。 
9 「DC 開発 166 件を一時停止、全国的な基準策定へ」『NNA ASIA』、2026 年 9 月 7 日。

美 
國 
美國就業及企業活動持續改善，期中選舉牽動財政與科技監管政策 
經濟 
⚫ 10 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數由 54.5 升至 55.5，連續第 10 個
月維持於擴張區間；新訂單、生產及庫存表現改善，顯示需求與生產動
能仍具韌性。惟投入成本仍處高檔，且關稅政策、地緣政治及供應鏈風
險猶存，後續發展仍有待進一步觀察。 
⚫ 10 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數為 54.5，較上月 54.9 略降 0.4 個
百分點，但已連續 28 個月維持擴張，服務業景氣持續增長。新訂單與
商業活動雖趨緩，就業續呈擴張，未完成訂單創 2022 年 7 月以來高點；
新出口訂單重返擴張區間，顯示內外需求仍具韌性，惟關稅、能源價格
及成本壓力仍為後續觀察重點。 
⚫ 10 月美國非農就業增加 16.5 萬人，高於上月修正後之 6.0 萬人，失業
率由 4.2%降至 4.1%，加以前兩個月就業人數合計上修 8.4 萬人，顯示
勞動市場較前期有所回溫。惟新增就業仍主要集中於專業及商業服務、
醫療保健及運輸倉儲等部分服務業，製造業增幅相對有限，反映勞動市
場雖維持韌性，但部分就業品質及企業用工需求仍存在壓力。 
⚫ 9 月美國職位空缺數由 719.0 萬個增至 752.3 萬個，職缺率由 4.3%升至
4.5%，企業徵才需求有所回升；聘僱人數亦同步增加，聘僱率由 3.3%
升至 3.4%。整體而言，企業用人需求改善、裁員規模下降，勞動市場仍
維持穩健，供需情勢持續朝平衡方向發展。 
⚫ 9 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差 1,050 億美元，較上月減少 0.7%。出口成
長 1.9%，高於進口增幅 1.2%，主因資本財及黃豆等農產品出口增加；
進口則受資本財及消費財需求帶動。累計 1 至 9 月貿易逆差較去年同期
縮減 12.2%，顯示對外貿易失衡情況有所改善。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 2026 年美國期中選舉呈現反執政黨效應，民主黨重掌國會主導權。截至
11 月 8 日開票結果，民主黨已確定取得參、眾兩院多數席次，其中參議
院獲 54 席、共和黨 44 席；眾議院則由民主黨取得 254 席、共和黨 162
席。州長席次方面，民主黨掌握 28 席，共和黨 20 席，惟部分選區仍在
計票，最終席次尚待確認。民主黨在多個關鍵州選戰中獲勝，反映選民
對高物價、能源價格及中東局勢等議題的不滿情緒升高。選後國會由民

115.11.9 
一、 美國—就業及企業活動持續改善，期中選舉牽動財政與科技監管政策 
2026 年 10 月美國非農就業人數增加 16.5 萬人，明顯高於上月修正後之 6.0
萬人，亦高於過去 12 個月平均每月增加 4.8 萬人之水準，企業聘僱動能有所回
升；失業率則由 4.2%降至 4.1%，持續維持於 3 月以來 4.1%至 4.3%之區間，勞動
市場整體仍具穩定性。產業別方面，新增就業主要由專業及商業服務業增加 7.0
萬人、醫療保健業增加 4.2 萬人及運輸倉儲業增加 2.5 萬人所帶動，製造業亦增
加 1.9 萬人，其餘產業就業變動幅度相對有限。薪資方面，私人部門平均時薪升
至 38.0 美元，月增 0.4%、年增 3.1%，增幅較前月略有加快；平均每週工時則維
持 34.4 小時不變。此外，8 月及 9 月新增就業人數合計上修 8.4 萬人，反映上兩
月勞動市場表現較初估時有所改善。整體而言，10 月美國新增就業明顯回升、失
業率小幅下降，加上前兩個月就業數據獲得上修，勞動市場有所改善，整體仍具
韌性；惟新增就業主要集中於部分服務業，且因經濟因素從事兼職工作的人數增
加，反映勞動市場仍存在就業不足及產業表現不均的情況。 
2026 年 9 月美國職位空缺數(Job Openings)由上月修正後之 719.0 萬個增加
33.3 萬個至 752.3 萬個，職缺率由 4.3%升至 4.5%，企業徵才需求略有回溫；聘僱
人數由 527.1 萬人增加至 540.2 萬人，聘僱率由 3.3%升至 3.4%。總離職人數由
517.5 萬人增加至 531.9 萬人，離職率維持於 3.3%。就離職結構觀察，主動離職
(Quits)人數由 304.4 萬人增加至 336.5 萬人，主動離職率由 1.9%升至 2.1%，勞工
轉職意願有所回升；裁員與解僱(Layoffs and Discharges)人數則由 176.8 萬人減少
至 164.1 萬人，裁員率由 1.1%降至 1.0%，企業人力調整壓力仍屬溫和。產業別方
面，職位空缺增加主要來自州及地方政府(不含教育)、專業與商業服務業、製造
業及營建業；聘僱人數則以住宿與餐飲服務業增幅較為明顯；醫療保健與社會救
助業裁員人數則有所下降。整體而言，9 月美國職缺數與聘僱人數同步增加，主
動離職意願回升且裁員規模下降，顯示企業用人需求有所改善，勞動市場仍維持
穩健，惟供需關係持續朝較為平衡方向發展。 
2026 年 10 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI)由上月的
54.5 升至 55.5，增加 1.0 點，連續第 10 個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，製造業景

氣持續擴張。細項觀察，新訂單指數由 55.3 升至 55.5，接單情況持續改善；生產

2026 年 10 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI)為 54.5，較上月
54.9 下降 0.4 個百分點，惟已連續第 28 個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，且高於近
12 個月平均值 54.3，美國服務業景氣持續擴張。細項觀察，商業活動指數由 56.5
降至 53.8，新訂單指數由 59.8 降至 55.9，顯示需求與營運動能較前月放緩，但仍
維持擴張；就業指數由 50.1 升至 51.4，連續第 2 個月處於擴張區間，反映企業用
人需求仍具韌性。供應商交貨指數由 53.2 升至 56.7，交貨時間進一步延長；價格
指數由 74.0 降至 73.0，雖較前月回落，但已連續 23 個月高於 60，企業成本壓力
仍高。庫存指數由 57.8 降至 55.4，未完成訂單指數則由 56.6 升至 58.0，創 2022
年 7 月以來最高，顯示需求累積情形持續增加。此外，新出口訂單指數由 46.9 大
幅升至 55.2，重返擴張區間，進口指數亦由 52.9 升至 54.2，顯示外部需求及採購
活動回溫。ISM 指出，關稅及燃料成本仍是企業最關注的供應鏈議題，許多企業
尚未完全將增加成本轉嫁給客戶，未來仍須持續關注關稅、能源價格及成本壓力
對服務業景氣與通膨之影響；惟值得注意的是，10 月所有主要指標均位於擴張區
間，為 2026 年 2 月以來首見，顯示整體服務業景氣基礎仍具支撐。

惟工業用品及原材料進口減少 37 億美元。累計 2026 年 1 至 9 月，美國商品及服
務貿易逆差為 6,623 億美元，較 2025 年同期減少-12.2%；同期出口成長 11.6%，
進口增加 6.2%，顯示對外貿易失衡情況較去年同期有所改善。 
2026 年美國期中選舉呈現反執政黨效應，民主黨重掌國會主導權，截至 2026
年 11 月 8 日，美國期中選舉開票結果顯示，民主黨已確定取得國會參、眾兩院
控制權。其中，參議院民主黨陣營取得 54 席，共和黨 44 席；眾議院民主黨取得
254 席，共和黨 162 席。此外，全美州長席次分布方面，民主黨掌握 28 席，共和
黨 20 席。由於部分選區仍在計票或尚未確認選舉結果，最終席次仍待確定。民主
黨並於全國眾議院普選票(Popular Vote)獲得較高得票率，且在德州、堪薩斯州、
北卡羅來納州、俄亥俄州及南卡羅來納州等關鍵州選戰取得突破，反映選民對高
物價、能源價格上漲、中東衝突及部分政策方向的不滿情緒升高。此次選舉終結
共和黨自 2024 年總統大選以來同時掌控白宮與國會兩院的局面，美國再度形成
「分裂政府」(Divided Government)格局。未來川普政府推動重要法案、預算案、
人事任命及稅制改革等政策，將面臨更大的國會制衡與協商壓力，民主黨亦可能
加強對行政部門的監督與調查。就經濟層面而言，市場預期大規模減稅、財政擴
張及部分貿易政策推動速度可能放緩，有助於降低財政赤字進一步擴大的風險；
惟兩黨對立加深，亦可能提高預算協商、債務上限及政府融資等議題的不確定性。 
美國民主黨於 2026 年 11 月期中選舉取得國會參、眾兩院控制權後，未來人
工智慧監管政策走向受到關注。其中，由無黨籍參議員桑德斯（Bernie Sanders）
及民主黨眾議員卡薩爾（Greg Casar）於 9 月分別提出的《2026 年禁止人工超級
智慧法案》（Ban Artificial Superintelligence Act of 2026），主張禁止開發、部署、
持有及資助具備超越人類認知能力或可能威脅人類安全的人工超級智慧（ASI），
並設立內閣層級的聯邦「人工智慧部」（Department of Artificial Intelligence），
統籌先進 AI 系統的安全審查及監管。此外，法案規劃在監管機構完成籌設並建
立相關安全規範前，暫停訓練運算量達 10²⁵次以上的先進 AI 系統進行訓練、修改
及微調，並限制未發布系統的部署。截至 11 月 8 日，該法案仍處於國會委員會
審議階段，尚未正式立法。隨著民主黨將於 2027 年 1 月取得國會主導權，後續
法案審議進展及其對美國 AI 技術發展、企業投資與國際競爭的影響，值得持續
關注。

二、歐元區—通膨壓力持續增強 
 歐元區 10 月 HICP 通膨上升 0.2 個百分點至 4%，核心通膨(不含食品及能源)
則持平為 2.5%。該月食品及能源通膨皆持續上揚，反映國際原油價格居高不下，
並透過肥料、物流成本上漲，間接推升食品價格。另外，服務通膨上升 0.1 個百
分點至 3.3%，部分反映交通費用上漲的影響，惟服務通膨仍是低於今年上半年的
平均水準。展望未來，近期布蘭特原油雖回落至 100 美元內，但美伊談判陷入僵
局，仍預期年底前布蘭特原油位於每桶 90 美元以上，將對通膨帶來額外壓力，後
續須關注能源漲價可能會透過運輸、物流等成本管道傳導至核心商品及服務價格，
進而提高核心通膨黏性。 
 其他經濟數據包含：(1)歐元區 9 月失業率上升 0.1 個百分點為 6.5%，失業人
數增加 12.2 萬人，主要是法國失業人數增加較多所致，係因大規模抗議活動的影
響；(2)歐元區 9 月零售額月減 0.7%、第三季季減 0.5%，物價上漲帶來的負面影
響逐步顯現，預期第四季零售額將延續此一疲態。 
三、日本—薪資成長動能增強，春鬥效應持續擴散 
 9 月薪資年增 4.6%，創 1997 年以來最大增幅，除受夏季獎金大增 32.6%支撐
外，經常性薪資亦年增 3.7%，顯示今年春鬥的加薪成果正逐步由大型企業擴散至
中小企業。隨企業獲利保持強勁，加上日本最大工會聯合組織（Rengo）已表態明
年將爭取更高加薪幅度，預期薪資成長動能仍可延續，有助支撐民間消費，但同
時也可能推升服務價格，增加通膨持續性。 
四、中國—關注 11 中旬川習會發展 
10 月官方製造業 PMI 下降 0.8 點至 49.3，主因十一長假前需求提前釋放，以
及外部環境不確定性升高所致。新訂單、生產指數分別降至 49.4、49.8，新出口
訂單亦降至 48.7。此外，生產及出廠價格指數分別降至 56.8 及 51.7，反映成本與
售價漲幅已有收斂跡象；惟就業指數則持平於 48.4，顯示就業仍顯疲弱。不過，
RatingDog 製造業 PMI 略增 0.1 點至 52.1，反映 RatingDog 調查以中小型出口企
業為主，在全球 AI 相關需求的支持下，仍能保持穩定的擴張表現；惟 RatingDog
製造業產出物價指數連續兩個月下調，投入物價指數則持續上揚，反映競爭環境
激烈，迫使廠商犧牲利潤以降價求售。

8

官方非製造業 PMI 下降 0.5 點至 49.7，其中建築業商務活動指數由 50.3 降至
48.0，服務業商務活動指數則小幅下降至 50，顯示十一長假消費動能並不特別活
躍；非製造業就業指數雖微升至 46.0，但仍遠低於榮枯線，不利於後續內需復甦。
另外，RatingDog 服務業指數下降 0.8 點至 50.8，亦反映需求增速減弱，使得
RatingDog 綜合產出指數為 52，較 9 月的 52.4 回落，為近三個月最慢，製造業產
出增快為能抵銷服務業擴張放緩。 
展望未來，中國製造業仍面臨外需不確定、內需復甦偏弱及價格競爭激烈等，
惟全球 AI 產業需求可望擴張，加上人行上調外匯存款準備率，有助緩和人民幣
升值對出口競爭力的壓力，接有助於繼續支持出口需求。此外，若 11 月 APEC 期
間川習會釋出改善中美經貿關係訊號，將有助提振企業信心及出口前景，惟後續
仍須觀察全球需求及地緣政治發展。 
五、印度及東南亞—馬來西亞央行按兵不動；印尼、越南出口亮眼；泰國通膨壓
力上升 
(1) 印尼：9 月出口額達 275.9 億美元，創四年新高、年增 11.87%，非油氣出口較
去年成長 12.39%，以動植物油脂成長 89.04%為最，礦物燃料及電氣機械及設
備分別成長 12%、24.37%，抵銷石油和天然氣出口下滑 1%的影響，對美國、
中國出口分別成長 23.21%、11.82%。9 月進口額達 242.3 億美元、年增 18.41%，
增幅創 6 個月新低，其中成品油進口成長 37%，非石油和天然氣進口額年增
17.59%，以電氣機械設備(25.9%)及塑膠製品(16.3%)增幅較大，自中國進口額
大增 30.23%、自東協和歐盟進口分別成長 20.98%、18.21%。總計 9 月，貿易
順差為 33.6 億美元，2026 年前 9 個月，貿易順差 106.1 億美元，出口和進口
分別成長 5.58%和 19.67%。 
(2) 泰國：10 月消費者物價年增率(CPI)從 9 月的 2.82%飆升至 3.9%，3 年多來首
次突破泰國央行 1%-3%的目標區間，主要受油價上漲和洪水導致食品價格上
漲的影響，泰國商務部預計 2026 年整體通膨率平均在 1.8%-2.2%之間，但由
於基期較低，物價壓力可能在年底前持續走高。雖然通膨壓力顯著增強，但官
方評估此波物價飆漲應屬暫時現象。 
(3) 馬來西亞：馬來西亞央行(BNM)11 月會議將隔夜政策利率（OPR）維持在 2.75%
不變，為連續第八次維持利率不變，符合市場預期。政策聲明強化對經濟成長

的樂觀評估，表示在強勁的出口表現和持續的內需支撐下，2026 年全年經濟
成長率有望突破 5%，預計 2027 年經濟活動在科技出口帶動下將保持韌性。
通膨方面，央行指出地緣政治發展、大宗商品價格上漲及不利天氣狀況將對通
膨構成上行壓力，但現有的燃油和公用事業補貼應有助於抑制通膨。整體而言，
央行目前政策立場仍支持物價穩定且可持續的經濟成長，並樂觀看待未來經濟
及通膨前景的發展。 
(4) 越南: 10 月出口額年增 51.4%，達 646.7 億美元的歷史新高，其中電子產品出
口年增 102.6%為主要成長動力，機械設備出口年增 49.8%居次。同時，10 月
進口額達 573.6 億美元、年增幅略放緩至 43.1%，其中，電子產品進口成長
84.5%，能源進口則因油價上揚而大幅增加 74.1%。總計 10 月，貿易順差為
73.1 億美元，連續第二個月轉為順差；2026 年前 10 月，貿易逆差小幅收斂至
120.7 億美元，出口額成長放緩至 27.5%、進口額成長率為 37.4%。10 月 CPI
年通膨率小幅上升至 5.09%，創 5 月以來最高，其中，以交通運輸費用上漲
15.57%為最，酒精飲料和菸草（4.40%）、住房和公用事業（6.50%）及醫療保
健（1.75%）價格漲幅均有所加快，由於通膨已連三月突破央行目標區間，將
加大升息的壓力。 
六、台灣—通膨漲幅收斂，尚無輸入性通膨疑慮 
10 月消費者物價指數（CPI）年增 2.33%，較 9 月的 2.74%明顯回落，顯示整
體通膨壓力已有降溫跡象，但仍近半年皆高於 2%。10 月物價漲幅收斂，主要受
蔬菜價格轉跌、電價漲幅縮小，以及部分商品與服務價格漲勢趨緩等因素影響，
惟外食費年增 3.01%，已連續 5 個月超過 3%，民眾對物價上漲的感受仍較明顯。
另，10 月以新台幣計價的進口物價年增 33.32%，創近 50 年最大漲幅，主要受國
際原油及 AI 需求推升電子零組件價格所致，惟相關電子零組件多供出口使用，
對國內消費端影響有限，加以政府採取油價平穩措施，因此主計總處認為目前並
無輸入性通膨問題。展望未來，隨中秋節比較基期因素消退，主計總處預估 11 月
CPI 年增率仍將高於 2%，且漲幅可能較 10 月擴大。

ab 19 November 2026
Global Research
Gudeng Precision Industrial
Broad-based 3Q beat sets up a solid 2027
Solid Q3 earnings beat; momentum continuing into Q4 and 2027
Gudeng’s Q326 results came in above consensus, with revenue 5% and EPS 16% above
VA consensus. The beat was broad-based, driven by stronger revenue, gross margin, and
opex leverage. We expect momentum to continue into Q426, with full-year 2026
revenue growing 34%, vs our prior estimate of 29%, driven by continued strength in

EUV and China FOUP. Q4 gross margin should sustain at 46% level, with better margin
of EUV mask pod. Looking into 2027, we estimate revenue growth of 31%, primarily
volume-driven, with upside if Gudeng can add more capacity for FOUP. Of note,
advanced packaging revenue could potentially reach NT$0.85bn, up meaningfully from
~NT$0.3bn in 2026. Below the line, we estimate FY27 gross margin of 46%, with
favorable mix from EUV and China FOUP, while the opex ratio should improve to 24%

from 25% in 2026.
EUV mask pod sales to grow 30% in 2027
EUV demand has remained consistently strong throughout 2026 and the sales should
grow 43% this year and 30% in 2027. Key drivers are the meaningful leading edge
expansion at TSMC, and Gudeng’s share gain in Intel. We estimate Gudeng’s EUV mask
pod shipment to Intel to likely close to triple YoY this year. DUV mask pod sales should
also grow 15-20% in 2027, supported by stable demand from China logic customers.

Meaningful recovery in China FOUP lifting ASP and gross margin
On top of the EUV strength, the business recovery in China FOUP is equally important in
re-accelerating Gudeng’s earnings and margin improvement. Gudeng has seen strong
demand from more diversified client base across logic and memory YTD, versus in the
past when growth was more reliant on tier 1 logic clients like SMIC. Considering the
tight supply at global peers, we anticipate the growth of China FOUP to sustain and

support 40% foup revenue growth in 2027. On TSMC side, we believe Gudeng should
also be improving product mix through 2027 with the upgraded diffuser wafer foup
with higher ASP and margin for N2. On advanced packaging, Gudeng is set to ramp up
sales more meaningfully from NT$300mn this year to NT$0.85bn in 2027, mostly
through large panel FOUP for substrate makers, but also good volume for CoWoS
related FOUP.
Valuation: Maintain Buy with lower price target of NT$610 from NT$710

Post Q3 results, we upgraded 2026/27 EPS by 14% and 2%. We lower our price target
from NT$710 to NT$610 based on a lower multiple of 26x 27-28E PE from 30x
previously, considering the broader sector de-rating and lower LT earnings CAGR of
26% from 29%. Maintain Buy.

Company Description
Gudeng Precision Industrial was founded in 1998 and entered semiconductor front-end
process supply chain in 2000. It manufactures reticle and wafer carriers,which provide
protection, delivery and storage solutions; it also supplies related equipment. According to

the company, it is the largest supplier of reticle carriers with a 70% market share globally and
holds an 85% market share for EUV POD, while its FOUP is adopted by over 50% of
customers in Greater China as the baseline.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Gudeng based on PE methodology.
Downside risk for the semis industry includes: 1) slower-than-expected N2 migration, 2)
geopolitical uncertainties that weigh on the semis cycle, and 3) meaningful deceleration of

new technology deveopment like Cloud AI, autonomous driving etc.
Downside risk for the company itself includes: 1) slower-than-expected share expansion in
FOUP market, 2) executions on pushing new products like diffuser FOUP, high NA EUV pods.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

精測(6510 TT) Buy

精測受惠 HPC 測試需求，2027 年 營 收、獲 利 皆 可 望 再 創 新
高 ， 給 Buy 的 投 資 評 等 。  
投 資 建 議：精 測 受 惠 於 HPC(TPU、AI5 等 )測 試 需 求，2027 年營
收 、 獲 利 皆 可 望 再 創 新 高 ，給 Buy 的 投 資 評 等 。  
精測 3Q26 營 收 、 獲 利 皆 創 單 季 新 高 ： 精測 3Q26 營收 19.05 億
元，QoQ+16.17%\YoY+53.35%；毛 利 率 57.26%、營 益 率 35.21%；
稅後純益 5.51 億 元，QoQ+11.61%\YoY+100.03%；稅 後 EPS 16.76
元。3Q26 營 收 、 獲 利 皆 創 單 季 新 高 ， 雖 然 因 新 產 品 開 始 認 列 營
收 ， 毛 利 率 略 為 季 減 ， 但 獲 利 成 長 還 是 大 於 營 收 成 長 。  
精 測 有 TPU 與 AI5 商機： 精 測 表 示 ， 美 系 客 戶 的 TPU 供 應 商
維 持 既 有 架 構 ， 預 期 在  TPU8 專 案 中 將 扮 演 一 定 角 色 ， 不 過 目
前 尚 未 正 式 發 包，相 關 專 案 預 計 於 2H26 啟 動。此 外，針 對 另 一
客戶的 AI5 專 案， 依 客 戶 規 劃 時 程， 預 計 06/2026 進 行 工 程 卡
交 付 ， 並 預 計 於 2026 年底至 2027 年 初 進 入 量 產 。  
精 測 新 產 能 持 續 開 出：2026 年 營 運 逐 季 成 長 趨 勢 明 確，3Q26 營
收 有 機 會 雙 位 數 季 增 ， 08/2026 、 03/2027 會 有 比 較 大 的 產 能 開
出 。 目 前 交 期 約 5~8 周 ， 新 產 能 客 戶 驗 證 時 間 約 需 一 季 ， 2027
年 營 運 健 康 成 長 。

### Page 2

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 11 月 24 日

2 群益投顧 
  ESG：  
在 環 境 保 護 方 面 ， 中 華 精 測 於 2022 年 開 始 自 主 性 導 入 溫 室 氣 體 盤 查 作 業 ，
除 了 計 算 碳 使 用 總 量 外，也 了 解 如 何 節 能 及 尋 找 替 代 能 源，並 推 動 各 項 環 保
節 能 減 碳 措 施、減 少 溫 室 氣 體 排 放 量 等，以 期 減 緩 與 調 適 氣 候 變 遷 所 帶 來 之
營 運 衝 擊 。  
在 社 會 責 任 方 面 ， 中 華 精 測 遵 守 相 關 法 規 及 國 際 人 權 公 約 ， 持 續 營 造 友 善 、
平 等、自 由 的 工 作 環 境，對 於 提 升 員 工 福 祉 不 遺 餘 力，且 不 定 期 舉 辦 家 庭 活
動 及 獎 勵 同 仁 結 婚 、 生 育 、 旅 遊 津 貼 等 員 工 福 利 。  
在 公 司 治 理 營 運 績 效 方 面 ， 中 華 精 測 連 續 兩 年 於 公 司 治 理 評 鑑 排 名 前 5%，
並於 2022 年 榮 獲 上 櫃 公 司 治 理 評 鑑  Top5%之 佳 績，秉 持 誠 信 正 直 原 則，定
期 宣 導 及 教 育 訓 練 內 線 交 易、誠 信 廉 潔 等 法 律 知 識，確 保 公 司 員 工 遵 守 誠 信
經 營 守 則 之 落 實 。  
公 司 簡 介 ：  
中 華 精 測 科 技 股 份 有 限 公 司 (6510 TT)設 立 於 08/2005，總 部 及 生 產 工 廠 皆 位
於 桃 園 平 鎮，為 中 華 電 信 所 屬 關 係 企 業，前 身 為 中 華 電 信 研 究 所 內 部 之 高 速
PCB 團 隊 ，2001 年 起 開 始 嘗 試 研 發 設 計 測 試 載 板 (Load board) 及 探 針 卡 載 板
(Probe PCB) 。  
中 華 精 測 主 要 從 事 晶 圓 測 試 板 、 IC 測 試 板 、 技 術 服 務 與 其 他 等 業 務 ， 提 供
半導體產業測試所需的介面板服務，用於晶圓測試之探針卡專用印刷電路
板 (Probe PCB) ， 及 用 於 IC 測 試 之 載 版 (Load Board) 。  
中 華 電 信 (2412 TT) 持股中 華 投 資 公 司 約 89.0% ； 中 華 投 資 持 股 精 測 約
33.6%；聯發科(2454 TT)於 2015 年，透 過 旗 下 翔 發 投 資 公 司 入 股 中 華 精 測，
目 前 持 股 約 0.5%。  
精 測 的 台 灣 同 業 包 含 旺 矽 (6223 TT)、穎崴(6515 TT)、 雍 智 科 技 (6683 TT)…
等。精 測 的 客 戶 包 含 美 系 HPC 公 司、台 系 IC 設 計 公 司、美 系 IC 設 計 公 司，
台 系 晶 圓 代 工 廠 …等。 
營 收 占 比 _產 品 別 ：  
產 品 目 前 毛 利 率 排 序 ： 整 組 探 針 卡 >FT 用的 loadboard>SLT 板 / 老化板。 
晶 圓 測 試 卡 包 含 PCB 與 整 組 探 針 卡 。

3 群益投顧 
資 料 來 源 ： 精 測 法 說 會 簡 報 。  
營 收 占 比 _產 品 應 用 別 ：

資 料 來 源 ： 精 測 法 說 會 簡 報 。  
精測 4Q26 毛 利 率 可 能 季 減 ：  
精 測 之 前 說 3Q26 毛 利 率 區 間 為 50~55%，但 實 際 毛 利 率 優 於 預 期，因 09/2026
才 開 始 出 毛 利 率 較 差 的 老 化 板，所 以 對 3Q26 毛 利 率 影 響 有 限，精 測 估 4Q26
毛利率 50~55%， 但 還 是 要 看 產 品 組 合 。  
精 測 估 2027 年 營 收 、 獲 利 雙 位 數 成 長 ：  
精測估 1Q27 營 收 將 介 於 3Q26~4Q26 間，精 測 表 示 以 目 前 訂 單 的 狀 況，2027
年 營 收 維 持 50%的 成 長 ， EPS 的 成 長 也 是 可 以 期 待 。  
整 組 探 針 卡 於 往 年 的 合 併 營 收 占 比 約 20~30%，精 測 2027 年 挑 戰 占 比 30%，
但 也 要 看 其 他 產 品 的 成 長 性 。  
現 在 由 於 營 收 規 模 成 長，精 測 建 議 費 用 率 預 估 為 20~25%，用 於 研 發、留 才、
與 國 際 大 廠 的 合 作 等 。  
TPU 工 程 驗 證 產 品 預 計 11/2026 開 始 出 貨 ：  
傳聞 TPU 產 品 因 封 裝 問 題 ， 量 產 時 間 遞 延 ， 精 測 表 示 工 程 驗 證 產 品 的 出 貨
沒 有 遞 延 ， 量 產 時 間 預 計 會 是 1H27， 量 產 的 數 量 還 沒 有 確 定 。  
TPU 專 案，精 測 將 於 11/2026 開 始 出 貨 工 程 驗 證 用 的 整 組 探 針 卡，之 後 量 產
的 數 量 還 沒 確 定 。 記 憶 體 用 的 整 組 探 針 卡 也 會 於 2026 年 送 樣 。  
美 系 車 用 晶 片 大 廠 的 訂 單 ， 精 測 也 於 2026 年 開 始 出 貨 工 程 驗 證 的 產 品 ， 預
計將於 2027 年 量 產 ， 之 後 量 產 的 數 量 還 沒 確 定 。 工 程 驗 證 結 束 ， 到 正 式 量
產 通 常 需 要 1~2 季。 
除了 TPU 專 案 以 外 ， 精 測 也 積 極 驗 證 北 美 、 韓 國 客 戶 ， 精 測 估 2027 年 的 產
能 與 稼 動 率 皆 能 維 持 高 檔 。  
精 測 有 發 CB， 三 廠 產 能 預估 4Q28 開出：  
精測 07/29 重 訊 決 議 發 行 CB， 預 計 募 資 20 億 元 ， 主 要 用 於 新 廠 的 工 程 款 ，

4 群益投顧 
其 餘 用 於 營 運、新 材 料、新 產 品 的 研 發。 三 廠 預 計 4Q25 開 始 動 工、4Q28 貢
獻 營 收 ， 新 產 能 是 舊 產 能 的 100%以上。 
其 他 補 充 ：  
2026 年 CAPEX 從 3.5 億元提 高 到 5.0 億 元，用 在 擴 充 產 能、承 租 廠 房；2026
全 年 折 舊 金 額 約 4.1 億元。 
亞 馬 遜 雲 端 服 務 與 高 通 (QCOM US) 於 09/2026 宣 布 建 立 長 期 合 作 關 係，精 測
表 示 ， 可 能 透 過 晶 圓 代 工 廠 、 IC 設 計 服 務 公 司 來 取 得 相 關 的 訂 單 。  
美 國 太 空 客 戶 的 訂 單 ， 精 測 有 與 日 本 Yokowo(6800 JP) 合 作 ， 精 測 出 板 子 ，
Yokowo 出 pogo pin 和 測 試 座 。  
2027 年 的 折 舊 、 CAPEX 預 計 之 後 開 實 體 法 說 再 說 明 金 額 。  
精測目前 MEMS 探 針 的 月 產 能 約 100 萬 pin。  
CPO 測 試 部 分 ， insertion 2 和 3， 用 到 的 板 子 會 比 較 多 。

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 11 月 24 日

8 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】  
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 ≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <15%

中立(Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等  
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整  
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】  
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較 3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為 四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等 係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。   
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」 及 「 區 間 操 作 」 均有 upside 目 標 價 。 差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。  
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ：  
1. 達 目 標 價 。  
2. 雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。  
3. 推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 ， 亦 將 出 具 降 評 報 告 。

【免責聲明】 
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

## Document 232: [034020] 두산에너빌리티(034020) 3Q26 Review_ 바닥을 잡는다면 지금두산에너빌리티(034020) 3Q26 Review_ 바닥을 잡는다면 지금3Q26 Review_ 바닥을 잡_62bfa2bd

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

Company Brief 
유틸리티/건설 
Analyst 문경원, CFA 
kyeongwon.moon@meritz.co.kr 
RA 이동석 
dongseok.lee@meritz.co.kr 3Q26 Review: 바닥을 잡는다면 지금  
믹스 개선에 따른 이익률 개선 조금씩 관찰 중 
3Q26 연결 영업이익은 2,612억원(+90.6% YoY)을 기록하며 컨센서스(2,455억원)
를 소폭 상회했다. 밥캣(1,674억원)이 컨센서스(1,761억원)를 소폭 하회한 가운데, 
에너빌리티 부문 영업이익(+179% YoY, OPM 6.6%)이 호실적을 이끌었다. 환율 하
락에도 불구, 가스터빈, 원전 등 고수익성 프로젝트의 비중 상승이 주요 원인이다. 
3Q26 누적 수주는 10.0조원(+85.5% YoY)을 기록했다. 4Q26 가스복합 EPC 수주 
등을 감안하면 연간 가이던스(13.3조원) 달성은 무난하다. 한수원과의 협상 결렬로 
이목이 집중된 체코 두코바니 시공 수주는 2027년 인식될 확률이 높아 보인다. 주간
사가 바뀌더라도 이미 프로젝트에 참여 중인 동사를 배제하기는 쉽지 않다.

대미투자 이후, 연말~연초 수주 모멘텀에 집중 
연말 연초에 다음과 수주 모멘텀이 집중되면서, 주가 상승을 이끌 전망이다. 
1) 대형원전: 정부 자금 중심의 Westinghouse LLI(장납기 기자재) 발주가 몰린다. 
우선 미국 DOE(에너지부)가 제공한 대출을 기반으로 미국 수주가 기대된다.  폴란드 
정부 역시 최근 LLI 발주 작업을 시작했다. 한편, 한국 정부는 연말까지 100억 달러 
규모의 대미 투자 송금을 협의 중인데, 이 역시 DOC(상무부) 주도의 대형 원전 건설
을 위한 LLI 발주에 쓰일 가능성이 높다 
2) SMR: 오래 기다려왔던 Nuscale 향 수주가 시작되고 있다. 우선 29일 모듈 1기 
제작 계약을 체결했다. 현금이 부족한 Nuscale이 용기를 냈다는 건, 곧 Nuscale-
TVA 간 PPA 가 임박했음을 의미한다. 본격적인 수주는 PPA 직후 예상된다.  
3) 가스 터빈: 2026년 초 해외 수주(7기)는 2029년까지 인도 계획이다. 이후 2030
년 슬랏 예약을 완료하고 2031년 물량을 논의 중으로 보인다. 지금은 2030년 슬랏
에 대한 PO가 나와 수주잔고에 인식할 타이밍이다. 역시 수출 물량으로 추측한다.

성장에 대한 기대가 사라진 밸류에이션, 지금이 바닥 
투자의견 Buy를 유지, 할인율 상승을 반영하여 적정주가를 109,000원으로 -17% 
하향 조정한다. 금리 상승, 환율 하락, 트럼프 지지율 하락 등 Top-down 리스크에 
최근 조정을 받았지만, 추가적인 주가 하락 폭은 제한적일 전망이다. EV/선행 수주잔
고 지표는 현재 1.0배 수준까지 하락하며, 원전의 성장성이 주목받지 못했던 
2021~2024년 수준에 근접하게 내려왔다.  
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 30

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 109,000원

## Document 261: AI周期能持续多久？

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI周期能持续多久？.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⾼盛Eric Sheridan明确AI周期三⼤终结信号：终端需求萎缩、资本断流、电⼒短缺。认为
超⼤规模云⼚商15%回报率可期，周期需五⾄⼋年兑现，市场⽤六个⽉考核周期丈量⻓期
基建，幻灭难免。Social Capital创始⼈Chamath则警告，新兴云⼚商以短期算⼒租赁收⼊
偿还⻓期债务，GPU利⽤率若⽆法突破75%将引发债务危机。
摘要：
2026年11⽉，美国中期选举尘埃落定，美股AI板块的剧烈震荡与信贷市场的信⽤利差⾛阔，正
在把华尔街拉⼊⼀场关于“AI投资周期持久性”的深度拷问。
11⽉25⽇，⾼盛最新⼀期播客中，主持⼈ Allison Nathan 邀请了⾝处算⼒基建与企业软件⼀线
的Social Capital创始⼈Chamath Palihapitiya，以及⾼盛⾸席美国互联⽹研究分析师Eric
Sheridan。
两位嘉宾围绕新兴云⼚商的商业漏洞、企业端迟迟未兑现的投⼊产出⽐（ROI），以及科技巨头
的财务兜底能⼒，完整拆解了本轮AI周期的真实账本与潜在拐点。
⾼盛分析师Eric Sheridan认为超⼤规模云⼚商回报率可轻松超过15%红线，但明确三⼤周期终

结信号：终端需求萎缩、资本断流、电⼒短缺。
Social Capital创始⼈Chamath Palihapitiya警告，新兴云⼚商本质是"反向银⾏"，以短期算⼒租
赁收⼊偿还⻓期债务，GPU利⽤率若⽆法突破75%将引发债务危机。
他还指出，当前AI计费模式停留在"按Token收费"的1999年阶段，企业ROI尚未兑现。
算⼒基建的“时间差陷阱”：电⼒瓶颈与新兴云的反向银⾏危机
每⼀轮科技基建周期都会遭遇“建设前置”与“变现滞后”的久期错配，但在本轮AI浪潮中，这种错
配被物理世界的供电瓶颈和激进的⾦融杠杆急剧放⼤。
过去⼀年，市场对算⼒扩张的预期建⽴在芯⽚出货量的线性推演上，却严重低估了数据中⼼从
拿地到通电的物理阻⼒。
与此同时，⼀批依托资产⽀持证券（ABS）⼤举举债购买GPU的新兴云⼚商，正在复制航空业
的⾼危商业模型：背负⻓达六到⼋年的重资产折旧与融资债务，却在现货市场上按⼩时出售极
易贬值的算⼒座位。

### Page 2

⼀旦英伟达按年度节奏推出新⼀代芯⽚，或者⼤模型推理效率⼤幅提升，这些缺乏底层硬资产
壁垒的“算⼒⼆房东”将⾯临严峻的利⽤率考验。近期部分AI相关信贷产品的利差⾛阔和信⽤违
约互换（CDS）飙升，正是敏锐的债券市场提前嗅到了这种结构性脆弱。
Chamath Palihapitiya 过去⼀年将投资重⼼从芯⽚转向了底层的“⼟地、电⼒与机房外壳”，⽬前
已在全美积累了6吉⽡的电⼒资产。他在访谈中⽤⼀线实操经历点破了基建交付的残酷现实：
谈及新兴云⼚商的财务脆弱性，Chamath 给出了⼀个极具穿透⼒的⾦融类⽐：
他进⼀步警告称，就像航空公司上座率必须达到80%⾄85%才能盈亏平衡⼀样，新兴云⼚商的
GPU利⽤率必须跨越70%⾄75%的⻔槛才能在顶部25%的区间赚钱：
停留在“1999年”的商业模式：Agent越忙，企业越亏？
资本开⽀能否持续，最终取决于终端企业能否从AI中赚到真⾦⽩银。然⽽，当前AI产业的商业化
正卡在⼀个尴尬的瓶颈上：技术使⽤量在飙升，企业财务报表上的利润却纹丝不动。
这种背离源于计费模式与组织惯性的双重掣肘。

在计费层⾯，主流⼤模型⼚商依然停留在按“百万Token”售卖算⼒库存的阶段，企业买到的是计
算量⽽⾮业务结果。
在组织层⾯，企业中层员⼯普遍担忧AI会取代⾃⼰的岗位，导致新技术在⼤型组织内部的渗透远
慢于实验室预期，即便局部岗位节省了⼯时，这些零散的时间也往往在冗⻓的业务流程中被⽩
⽩耗散，未能转化为公司整体的全要素⽣产率。
Chamath创办的企业级AI公司8090专⻔⽤于观察AI如何转化为实际回报，他⽤⾃家公司的真实
账本揭⽰了这⼀悖论：
要把这些电⼒资产真正通电运转，其中的复杂程度简直令⼈⿇⽊。德克萨斯州的矿产权纠纷、因
为地下有个化粪池⽽卡住的环境评估、尺⼨不合的混凝⼟基座，再加上燃⽓轮机供应商的严重积
压。把这些因素拼在⼀起，现实情况让我⾮常担忧。我看到的是市场对可⽤产能做出了海量承
诺，但实际交付量却很低，⽽能产⽣实际投资回报的商业⾯积更是少之⼜少。
新兴云⼚商本质上就是⼀家反向运转的银⾏。传统银⾏吸收的是储⼾随时可能提⾛的短期存款，
发放的是⼗年、⼗⼀年期的按揭和商业贷款，也就是‘短借⻓贷’；新兴云⼚商把这套逻辑彻底颠

倒了过来，他们的收⼊⾼度依赖客⼾按⼩时计费的短期投机性需求，但背负的债务却是⻓期且固
定的。
如果你只是⼀堆租赁合同的集合体，本质上你就是⼀个中间商。未来18个⽉，如果⼤企业不能
从实验阶段转向⼤规模真实消费，Token消耗量就⽆法爆发，利⽤率就会卡在75%到85%的灰⾊
地带，久期错配的债务问题迟早会反噬。
如果你把8090内部的智能体⼯作流画成图表，你会看到Token消耗量直线上升，Agent的循环调
⽤次数⾼得离谱。我公司⾥⼲活的Agent数量⼤概⽐⼈类员⼯多出⼀个数量级，⽽且还在涨。但
如果你问我，今天的投资回报率（ROI）⽐昨天⾼了1%吗？我的回答是⼤概没有。事实上，我

在他看来，2026年的AI⾏业像极了1999年的互联⽹泡沫前夜：
他同时指出，翻遍当下美股上市公司的财报，每股收益（EPS）的增⻓⼤多来⾃股票回购，利
润率的改善多源于通胀带来的定价权，真正敢公开归功于AI驱动的盈利增⻓公司“少之⼜少，⼏
乎不存在”。
15%回报率红线与周期的三⼤刹⻋⽚
⾯对资本市场对“巨额烧钱”的质疑，⾼盛⾸席美国互联⽹研究分析师Eric Sheridan从历史计算周
期的规律出发，给出了相对冷静的回应。
在华尔街看来，过去12到18个⽉AI板块经历的三次显著回调，本质上是投资者试图⽤“六个⽉的
财报考核周期”，去丈量需要“五到⼋年才能兑现”的底层计算基建。
虽然内存等硬件成本出现了超预期的通胀，企业端普及也存在员⼯抵触的摩擦，但超⼤规模云
⼚商⼿握的巨额积压订单正在提供可量化的回报可⻅度。
Eric Sheridan 明确指出，市场当前的焦虑完全可以理解，但并未脱离历史轨道：
针对投资者最关⼼的投⼊资本回报率，Eric 披露了⾼盛内部的测算逻辑与最新财报季的验证信
号：

的利润率反⽽下降了1%，因为更多的Agent在跑更多的任务，导致我的总⽀出增加了，哪怕单
个Token的成本正在下降。
1999年，雅虎、AOL这些互联⽹巨头的核⼼商业模式是按千次展⽰（CPM）卖⼴告；到了2026
年，AI巨头的核⼼商业模式是按百万Token收费，两者如出⼀辙。直到三四年后，⾕歌彻底改变
了游戏规则，从按展⽰收费转向按点击或按转化付费（CPA），这意味着客⼾从‘购买库存’转向了
‘购买结果’。
我们今天依然坐在2026年买着以百万量计的Token。谁能率先在规模化层⾯转向‘按结果付费’，
谁就能让像我这样的企业主拿到财务团队算得清的账单，⽐如说‘你这个⽉花了100万美元，但带
来了150万美元的产出，回报率极佳，继续投⼊’。但今天，我还做不到这⼀点。
我分析过的每⼀个计算周期都会经历这个过程。随着周期向纵深推进、投资规模不断滚⼤，市场
审查必然会变得越来越严厉。⼀旦资本投⼊的时间点与回报可⻅度之间出现久期错配，幻灭感就
会蔓延到整个周期中，我们现在正处在这个阶段。
云业务、移动计算、桌⾯计算通常都需要五到⼋年的周期，但⼤多数⼈总希望它们按六个⽉的节

奏兑现。投资者渴望像管理实时库存⼀样去调配前瞻性的算⼒资本，但这在现实中根本⾏不通
——你必须先买地、建数据中⼼、塞满计算设备并点亮通电，然后才能把算⼒卖给别⼈。
我们围绕⾏业最低15%的投⼊资本回报率做过测算，我们认为超⼤规模云⼚商⾮常容易达到15%
以上的回报。甚⾄不需要100%的积压订单转化为收⼊，也不需要在此基础上产⽣额外增⻓，就
能实现这⼀回报⽔平。

Eric划出了三条决定AI投资周期⽣死的底线：
谁在为万亿豪赌兜底？
如果AI基建的回报兑现⽐预期更漫⻓，谁将最终承担资产负债表受损的代价？
两位嘉宾在⻛险定价上达成了⾼度⼀致：⻛险正沿着产业链条向外围扩散，拥有强⼤核⼼现⾦
流的科技巨头依然稳坐钓⻥台，⽽⾼度依赖外部融资和单⼀算⼒租赁的边缘玩家正在被市场重
新定价。
Chamath 通过AI梳理了所有上市云⼚商与新兴云⼚商的监管⽂件，将它们的产能建设承诺与“照
付不议（Take-or-pay）”债务进⾏了交叉⽐对。他发现，绝⼤多数新兴云⼚商的特殊⽬的载体
（SPV）背后，其实都站着提供投资级信⽤增级的科技巨头：
Chamath认为科技巨头最终是对的，但问题只在于“什么时候”。Chamath说：
Eric Sheridan则从⼆级市场估值的⻆度印证了这⼀⻛险分层：
在刚刚过去的财报季，微软和亚⻢逊都针对投⼊资本回报率给出了⾮常详尽的解答，两家公司的
股价都在财报后实现了双位数上涨；Alphabet 明确表⽰其云业务回报率远⾼于加权平均资本成

本；Meta 的营收增速更是保持在25%左右，⽽12个⽉前我曾预测他们本季度增速会降⾄12%，
正是AI在核⼼⼴告业务中的应⽤为⼴告主创造了实打实的收⼊增量。
⼤家总想让我从股价的⻆度回答这个问题，但周期放缓绝不会是⼀个由股价驱动的决定。超⼤规
模云⼚商承受股价阵痛的忍耐周期，远⽐我每天接触的绝⼤多数机构投资者要⻓。
真正可能阻断或推迟这⼀主题演进的只有三件事：终端需求、资本获取能⼒以及电⼒供应。
只要电⼒跟不上、资本断了流、或者需求塌了⽅——这三块拼图只要缺了任何⼀块，我们就会陷
⼊⼀种更永久性的停滞，⽽不仅仅是⼀次短暂的幻灭低⾕。
所有路径最终都指向了为这场基建狂潮提供资⾦源头的投资级巨头，也就是超⼤规模云⼚商。有
意思的是，这些⼤块头已经从过去⾃由现⾦流极其充沛、忙着回购股票、⼏乎不发债的状态，变
成了⾃由现⾦流⼤幅转负，开始发债甚⾄发⾏股票来为基建融资。他们押上了全部⾝家，坚信⾃
⼰能为这场建设兜底。
就像当年互联⽹泡沫⼀样，所有关于⽣产⼒和收⼊增⻓的预测最终都应验了，但⼤家全都在时间

点上犯了错——市场从2002年⼀路出清到2007年，紧接着⼜碰上全球⾦融危机，整个⾏业直到
2010、2011年才真正站稳脚跟。不过，超⼤规模云⼚商不会有事，他们拥有极其扎实的核⼼业
务和近乎完美的资产负债表。

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

above the central bank’s target, strengthening the case for further
tightening. While lower fuel prices should ease headline inﬂation in
the months ahead, food remains a key concern in India. It poses
upside risks to inﬂation expectations and keeps underlying price

Philippines: Headline inﬂation to ease as
fuel prices fall
Philippine CPI inﬂation should ease further, reﬂecting sharp declines
in fuel prices. Gasoline and diesel have recorded consecutive weekly
cuts, with average gasoline prices down around 11% in November
versus October, providing meaningful drag on headline inﬂation.
Core inﬂation, however, is likely to remain relatively sticky,

demand.
China: Manufacturing to improve but remain
in contraction
China published November purchasing managers’ index data.
We’re looking for the manufacturing PMI to rebound to 49.5 in
November, up from 49.3, but remain in contraction territory.
Industrial production cooled slightly in October. Ideally, we’d see
better momentum in November. The non-manufacturing PMI is
expected to fare slightly better, coming in at 49.7, stable from

October.
Taiwan: Local elections in focus
No signiﬁcant data releases are expected for Taiwan in the week
ahead. However, local elections over the weekend will be the key
event to watch. Though these elections may often be driven more
by local issues than national politics, they will still serve as a check
on relative support for the Democratic Progressive Party (DPP) and
Kuomintang (KMT).
Korea: Trade strength and inﬂation in focus
South Korea will release October industrial production data on

Monday. The market expects annual growth to accelerate to 14.5%
year-on-year, from 1.2% in September. Month-on-month, output is
forecast to decline 0.6% after the previous month’s 9.0% increase.

November trade data are due on Tuesday. Exports and imports are

Japan: Retail sales, IP and capital spending
in focus
Japan releases October retail sales and industrial production data
on Monday. The market expects retail sales growth to rise to 2.7%
YoY, from a revised 2.4% in September. The improvement would
suggest that household consumption is recovering gradually,
although elevated living costs and higher borrowing costs remain
headwinds. Industrial production is expected to rise 2.1% MoM in
October, broadly in line with September’s 2.0% increase. Year-on-

year, the market expects industrial production growth to accelerate
to 2.7% from 1.7%, indicating that manufacturing activity remains
resilient.
Japan will also release third-quarter capital spending data on
Monday. The market expects capital spending growth to accelerate
to 4.3% YoY, from 1.6% in the second quarter. The expected pickup
would suggest that corporate investment remains resilient despite
higher borrowing costs.

## Document 291: Capital Market Outlook (2026-11-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Capital Market Outlook (2026-11-02).pdf
Report source: Merrill Lynch (CMO)
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook
November 2, 2026
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do
About It: As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably
above $6 per gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major
theme of 2026. Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event,

on track to be one of the strongest on record. The upshot: more extreme weather
from droughts to heavy rains, warmer temperatures globally, lower crop production in
commodity-exporting economies, and higher prices across major agricultural products.
Importantly, these pressures won’t be equally distributed and will largely depend on the
magnitude and timing of weather events. Among the most vulnerable will be heavily
import-dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa and parts of Asia, while developed

economies that count food as a smaller share of consumer spending should be better
insulated. For investors, the prospect of a very strong El Niño only further reinforces our
conviction in several structural trends including a return to higher inflation and higher
interest rates, elevated commodity prices amid heightened geopolitical risk and supply
chain shortages, and greater investment in energy infrastructure and supply chain
resilience. In portfolios, we favor commodities as both an inflation hedge and diversifier.

Market View—Another Tale of Market Rotation: October offered another reminder
that headline index returns do not always tell the full story. The S&P 500 ended the month
slightly lower, but the more muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market
leadership beneath the surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the
Artificial Intelligence (AI) trade, particularly across semiconductors and memory stocks. Yet
pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S. Equities.

Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market, with Value-
oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. While it remains too early to
determine whether the shift will prove durable, October’s rotation may reflect less a retreat
from the AI theme and more a recalibration of valuations and expected returns as market
leadership broadens. For investors, the speed of the shift reinforces the importance of
diversification as the bull market enters a potentially more mature and differentiated phase.

Thought of the Week—Reorienting Around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction
to Capital Goods: AI is reshaping the physical economy, and companies are reorienting
capacity and investment plans to meet surging demand for data centers, power and
infrastructure. Industrial firms are adding or reviving capacity, while data center construction
has increased more than 60% over the past year and is outpacing other major construction
categories. At the same time, robust capital goods orders and long lead times for inputs such

as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers highlight both the strength of demand
and the supply constraints the buildout faces. Taking stock of these signposts, our view
is reinforced that the AI investment cycle remains in its middle innings, creating potential
opportunities for companies supplying the equipment and infrastructure behind the buildout.

MACRO STRATEGY
Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do About It
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably above $6 per
gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major theme of 2026.
Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event, on track to be one

of the strongest on record. Below we answer frequently asked questions on El Niño,
the potential impact on the global economy and markets and, most importantly, how
investors can position portfolios accordingly.
What is El Niño, and what makes this time different? El Niño is the warm phase
of the “El Niño-Southern Oscillation,” a natural climate pattern occurring every two to
seven years. Simply put, El Niño refers to the warming of sea surface temperatures in

the eastern Pacific, historically leading to drier conditions in some areas (Southeast Asia,
Australia, Central America, southern Africa) and wetter conditions in others (southern
U.S., parts of Latin America) (Exhibit 1).
What makes this episode different? Should forecasts prove correct, this El Niño is on
track to be historic. The National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration’s latest
outlook assigns at least an 83% probability that El Niño’s strength during October-

December will exceed that of any previous episode since 1950. Already, its impact has
been felt via wildfires in Indonesia, the weakest monsoon season in India since 2015,1
and severe flooding from Paraguay to Peru. The upshot: more extreme weather, hotter
temperatures globally, lower crop production in commodity-exporting economies, and
higher prices across major agricultural products.
Exhibit 1: El Niño's Impact Varies Globally.
Geography Typical rainfall pattern Specific impacts

South Asia Weaker summer monsoon risk Lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation
Southeast Asia Drier Lower rice and palm oil production, wildfire risk, food inflation
United States Wetter in the south; warmer/drier in the north Southern flood risk; lower northern heating demand
Central America & Northern South America Drier Drought, lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation, lower hydropower output
Southern Africa Drier Weaker maize harvests, food inflation, lower hydropower output

Western South America Wetter along the Pacific coast Flooding, infrastructure damage, disrupted fisheries
Southern South America Wetter Flooding and crop damage; potential yield gains in some areas
Australia Drier Drought, agricultural losses, elevated wildfire risk, food inflation
Source: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration, World Meteorological Organization, Food and Agriculture Organization, Australian Bureau of Meteorology, World Bank. Data as of

October 30, 2026.
How will El Niño impact the global economy and markets? Which regions will be
most affected? El Niño’s impact on the global economy and markets is likely to be felt
most via commodity price inflation. Alongside higher energy and fertilizer costs resulting
from war in Europe and the Middle East, El Niño could put further upward pressure on
commodities like wheat, sugar, corn, soy, cocoa and coffee as drought (or extreme rain)
weighs on crop yields and production. Latest estimates from the United Nations Food

and Agriculture Organization (FAO) suggest global food prices were up 5.8% year-over-
year (YoY) in September (Exhibit 2). Livestock is also likely to be challenged by higher
temperatures globally, while flooding has already threatened copper production in
Chile. Meanwhile, with hydropower accounting for more than half of the average annual
electricity generation in Brazil, Colombia, Peru and other Latin American countries,2
below-average rainfall in affected areas has huge implications for electricity generation.

As an example, Colombia’s natural gas demand nearly doubled during the 2023-2024 El
Niño event.2 Another key watchpoint will be whether drier conditions reduce water levels
1 India Meteorological Department, Bloomberg. Refers to rainfall between June and September as of September 30, 2026.
2 International Energy Agency, as of October 7, 2026
Investment Implications
We expect El Niño to place upward
pressure on agricultural goods
prices globally. We continue to

see commodities as a strategic
diversifier and hedge against inflation
in portfolios. The U.S. remains our
preferred region with companies in
Industrials and Materials as potential
beneficiaries. Meanwhile, economic
impacts to the emerging world will vary
by country but the broader Emerging
Market (EM) equity market could be
less exposed given its significant tilt
toward Technology.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
in the Panama Canal and other key shipping routes, particularly in light of the Strait of
Hormuz closure.
These pressures won’t be equally distributed and will heavily depend on this El Niño’s
severity. Among the more vulnerable to higher agricultural prices will be heavily import-
dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa, and parts of Asia. So too will economies
that count food as a larger share of consumer spending such as the Philippines (36%),

Indonesia (33%), Egypt (33%), India (28%), and Vietnam (26%) (Exhibit 3). Developed
economies like the U.S. (6%), Canada (10%), and Germany (11%) stand to be better
insulated from a food-related shock to headline inflation.
Within the U.S., some impacts could be positive with a potentially milder winter reducing
heating costs and wetter conditions in the South improving agricultural conditions. A recent
paper from the Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco found that, in addition to putting

upward pressure on personal consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation, strong El Niño
episodes have been historically associated with a temporary boost to employment in the
following year via better construction conditions in the North and weather-event recovery
efforts.3 In California and parts of the Southwest, El Niño will be yet another stress test for
property insurers as flooding and landslide risks increase.
For more vulnerable developing economies, meanwhile, higher food and energy costs could

present a policy dilemma. On one hand, governments in vulnerable areas may move to
introduce fiscal relief to support households and boost growth. History points to plenty of
examples of this, such as Indonesia’s cash assistance in late 2023 or Thailand’s $1.8 billion
in loans to farmers in 2015. On the other hand, elevated inflation expectations and, in
some cases, weaker currencies could limit EM central banks’ room to ease monetary policy,
particularly with several major developed central banks aligned in hiking interest rates. Also

complicating matters: Global sovereign debt surpassed $110 trillion in Q2 2026, leaving
less fiscal space to absorb incoming shocks.4
How can investors position portfolios accordingly? In many ways, the prospect of a
very strong El Niño only reinforces structural trends we’ve identified as defining the coming
years: a return to higher inflation and higher interest rates; elevated commodity prices
amid heightened geopolitical risk and resource/supply shortages; and greater investment in

energy infrastructure and supply chain resilience.
Considering these themes, we continue to like commodities not just as a hedge against
higher inflation but also as a diversification tool in portfolios given their negative correlation
with Equities and bonds. That’s not to mention secular forces placing upward pressure on
commodity prices such as the resource-intensive AI buildout and broader protectionism. To
wit, the Bloomberg Commodity Index, which spans oil, gold, various metals and agricultural

goods has gained nearly 30% this year, handily outperforming the S&P 500 (+1%) and the
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index (-1%).
Within Equities, the U.S. is still our preferred region. While upside risk to inflation remains
a risk to watch going into 2027, food is a relatively small share of the consumer basket in
the U.S., and neither strong services spending nor capital investment should be materially
affected by El Niño. Companies within the Industrials and Materials sectors focused on

precision agriculture, water management and crop protection may be positioned to benefit.
Import-dependent EM economies remain particularly exposed, though impacts will vary
widely depending on the magnitude and timing of weather events. It’s worth noting too that
EM Equities remain heavily concentrated in East Asian markets with substantial exposure to
technology, leaving the broader index arguably less exposed to the economic ramifications
of El Niño than in past episodes. Indeed, even after recent weakness in semiconductor

names, Taiwan, Korea, and China together account for a near-70% of the broader MSCI
Emerging Markets Index.5 We maintain a slight overweight to the region in portfolios given
earnings strength but favor an active approach.

MARKET VIEW
Another Tale of Market Rotation
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
October offered another reminder that headline index returns do not always tell the
full story. The S&P 500 ended the month slightly lower, down almost 1%, but the more
muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market leadership beneath the
surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the AI trade, particularly

across semiconductors and memory stocks, with the Philadelphia Semiconductor Index
experiencing a 19% intramonth drawdown. The dominant concerns in recent months
have centered on the sustainability of AI infrastructure spending and whether the scale
of investment will ultimately generate sufficient returns. Those only intensified amid a
sharp rise in bond yields after the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield spiked above 5.3% as of
October 5 for the first time since 2002, raising scrutiny over AI financing conditions and

whether higher borrowing costs could increase hurdle rates for continued investment
and slow the pace of capital spending.
Yet the pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S.
Equities. Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market,
with Value-oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. Value defeated
Growth in October, with the Russell 1000 Value Index outperforming its Growth

counterpart by 10 percentage points, an outcome seen in only about 1% of months since
1979. The move extends Value's outperformance for the year, with the index up 23%
year-to-date compared with down almost 1% for Growth. Participation also improved.
The equal-weighted S&P 500 outperformed its market-cap-weighted counterpart by its
widest monthly gap since 2009 (Exhibit 4A), while the share of S&P 500 constituents
trading above their 200-day moving average rebounded to 62% from just 43% at the

end of September, and 76% of stocks outperformed the index for the month.

not possible to invest directly in an index.

The latest rotation is not the first attempt at a change in market leadership this year.
The more important question is what that shift represents: a fundamental reassessment
of the AI trade or another step toward broader, more mature market leadership?
Recent Q3 Technology earnings results have sharpened that debate. Investors are
increasingly looking beyond the scale of AI spending to companies’ ability to translate
that investment into durable growth and attractive returns. The result may be greater

dispersion not only between Growth and Value, but also among AI leaders as investors
become more discerning about which companies can justify the scale of their
investments.
Investment Implications
We continue to look for potential
missing opportunities and favor
diversification across and within asset
classes in a multi-asset portfolio.
In particular, we see potential
opportunities to broaden exposure
beyond AI leaders toward areas
where earnings, capital spending and

productivity trends are improving,
including Value, Small- and Mid-caps,
and select non-U.S. markets, particularly
EMs and Japan. As market leadership
evolves, maintaining diversified
exposures and periodically rebalancing
portfolios can help investors participate
in a broader set of possible return
drivers without becoming overly
dependent on any single theme.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
But greater selectivity does not necessarily signal the end of the AI investment cycle.
YoY growth rates may become harder to maintain after two years of rapid acceleration
as the comparison base rises, but absolute levels of AI capital spending remain elevated.
What matters more is whether spending translates into strong productivity and earnings
growth ahead and whether AI demand continues to support investment. October’s

volatility may therefore reflect less a retreat from the AI theme and more a recalibration
of valuations and expected returns, as other areas of the market begin to contribute
more meaningfully to market performance.
Several developments ahead will help determine whether that rotation has staying
power. One important test is whether broader participation can persist if pressure on AI
stocks continues. Continued strength outside the AI trade would support the case that

investors are reallocating capital rather than broadly reducing risk. The path of monetary
policy will also matter as investors assess where borrowing costs may be headed. The
Federal Reserve’s not-so-hawkish hold in October offered some reassurance, but the
path ahead remains dependent on the evolving balance between inflation and growth.
Any signs of resolution of geopolitical tensions remain an important swing factor
particularly as recent flashpoints in the Middle East have pressured energy prices higher

again. Results of the midterm elections should also provide greater visibility into the
policy landscape for the next few years. While the market does not need every clearing
event to resolve favorably, greater clarity around these uncertainties could help narrow
the range of potential outcomes facing investors.
For investors, October’s sudden change in leadership reinforces the importance of
diversification. Leadership can shift quickly, leaving portfolios that become overly

dependent on a single theme, sector or style particularly vulnerable when it does. We
remain constructive on U.S. Equities and believe that the market may be transitioning
more toward a mid-cycle phase in which returns become broader but also more
measured compared to the sharp acceleration earlier in the cycle. That argues not for
abandoning the market’s long-term winners, but for complementing them with exposure
to additional sources of returns across style, size, sector and region.

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Reorienting around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction to Capital Goods
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
For all the questions (and recent market weakness) hanging over the AI trade, from
monetization and cash burn to financing risk and overcapacity, a measurable aspect is
the buildout itself. Data center construction is lifting construction spending and capital

goods orders, while companies are adding and reviving capacity to meet AI-related
demand. Still, planning delays, access to power, labor scarcity, local politics and the cost
of capital remain meaningful constraints on the buildout’s pace or profitability.
Corporate Plans. Take Caterpillar as one example that has resumed production of a
10-megawatt natural gas turbine discontinued back in 2022. A 175-year old glassmaker,
Corning, is leaning into optical connectivity as AI clusters require faster connections of

optical fiber cabling between servers. Caterpillar and Corning are just two companies
among others pivoting to supply data centers.
Construction to Capital Goods. The AI reorientation is evident in construction
spending as well, where data center construction has outpaced every other major
residential and nonresidential category. Over the last year AI-related construction on data
centers has surged more than 60% while spending on residential, lodging, commercial

or office has declined notably (Exhibit 5A). Data center construction is running at an $85
billion (B) annualized rate this year, versus roughly $49B a year earlier, and now accounts
for 11% of private nonresidential construction, up from just 2% in 2022 according to
the Census Bureau. Similarly, demand for artificial intelligence business equipment and
transportation equipment drove an increase in durable goods orders in September on the
back of new orders for U.S.-manufactured capital goods, suggesting business spending

on equipment remained robust in Q3. Another factor, see Exhibit 5B, where critical
components such as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers continue to
face long lead times that affect delivery timelines and ultimate profitability for new data
center development.
These signposts reinforce our view that we are only in the middle innings of the AI
investment cycle. From an investment perspective, huge demand for data centers, power
systems, networking infrastructure, specialized chips and other equipment continues

to strain supply, creating both bottlenecks and potential opportunities for companies
powering the buildout.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500/Market-cap Weighted Index is a market-capitalization-weighted index that is widely regarded as the best single gauge of large-cap U.S. equities. The index includes 500 leading

companies and covers approximately 80% of available market capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index includes the same constituents as the capitalization weighted S&P 500, but each company in the S&P 500 EWI is allocated a fixed weight - or 0.2% of the
index total at each quarterly rebalance.
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index is a broad-based, market-capitalization-weighted benchmark that measures the performance of the U.S. investment-grade, U.S. dollar-denominated,

fixed-rate taxable bond market
Bloomberg Commodity Index is a broadly diversified financial benchmark that tracks the price movements of physical commodities through futures contracts.
MSCI Emerging Market Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets (EM) countries.
United Nations Food and Agriculture Food Price Index is a measure of the monthly change in international prices of a basket of food commodities.
Philadelphia Semiconductor Index is a market-cap-weighted index focusing on 30 leading semiconductor firms, offering insights into the tech market.

BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

## Document 341: Huanging On To The Bucking Bull

Source PDF: all report pdf/Huanging On To The Bucking Bull.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Huanging On To The Bucking Bull
Date:    9 hours ago
Huanging On To The Bucking Bull

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Huanging On To The Bucking
Bull
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 24 Nov 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Gilley Aguilar / Unsplash
I. Jensen Returns
In the 1984 sci-fi blockbuster, "The Terminator," Arnold Schwarzenegger
famously warned, "I'll be back." It took Jensen Huang just 11 days to make
his comeback. And it certainly was a blockbuster comeback!
Jensen's reinstatement as Nvidia's CEO on Monday triggered a
spectacular reversal of last week's stock market rout. The Nasdaq, S&P

### Page 3

500, and DJIA enjoyed their best day since President Trump announced a
90-day pause on most of his Liberation Day tariffs in April 2025. Nvidia
soared 31.5%, its biggest daily percentage gain in 23 years, adding a
record $950 billion to its market capitalization. Meanwhile, the
Philadelphia Semiconductor Index jumped 14.0%, its fourth-largest daily
gain since 1994. Neoclouds joined the rally, with CoreWeave and Nebius
both gaining more than 20%.
Some of that enthusiasm faded today. The Nasdaq 100 fell 2.0%,

remaining well below its 50-day and 200-day moving averages (charts).
The S&P 500 declined 0.5%, while its equal-weighted counterpart gained
0.5%, reflecting renewed rotation out of technology stocks. The
Philadelphia Semiconductor Index tumbled 7.9%, with Nvidia, AMD, and
Micron falling 5.9%, 8.9%, and 11.0%, respectively. Neoclouds IREN,
CoreWeave, and Nebius tumbled 11.7%, 11.9%, and 10.7%, respectively.

### Page 4

So, what changed? Consider the following:
(1) The Huang Man. On Monday, investors celebrated the reopening of
the AI trade's central bank. Under Jensen's leadership, Nvidia has eased
financial constraints on the AI buildout directly through capital injections
and financial backstops and indirectly through Jensen's votes of
confidence, which facilitate access to capital and lower borrowing costs
across the AI ecosystem.
Today, investors appeared to focus on the political ramifications of his

return. President Trump reportedly facilitated Jensen's reinstatement by
assuring Nvidia's board that he posed no national security threat, citing
the importance of America's technological leadership. However, the
decision has triggered bipartisan pushback, with Democratic Senator
Elizabeth Warren calling it "reckless" and Republican Senator John
Cornyn questioning the national security assurances.
In our view, Jensen's return could intensify the regulatory pressure on the

AI buildout already unleashed by the Democrats' midterm sweep,
emboldening China hawks to pursue tighter semiconductor export controls
and inviting greater congressional scrutiny of the AI ecosystem's business
practices and financing arrangements. So while Jensen's return as
Nvidia's CEO may have eased concerns about financial constraints on the
AI buildout, political constraints could intensify.
Nevertheless, we continue to view financial, political, and physical

constraints as speed bumps for the AI investment boom. They will slow

### Page 5

the buildout but won't brake it, as we expect demand for compute to
continue growing.
(2) Section 301. Reports broke today that the White House and USTR are
preparing to announce a fresh wave of Section 301 tariffs targeting
structural excess manufacturing capacity and state subsidies across 16
major economies—including China, the European Union, Japan, South
Korea, India, Vietnam, and Mexico.
(3) Export controls. Markets are also digesting the Commerce

Department's recent interim rule tightening export licensing requirements
on advanced AI chips to five countries (Singapore, Malaysia, Thailand,
Indonesia, and Brazil) and restricting overseas data centers from renting
substantial computing power to Chinese-headquartered firms. Yesterday
evening, the Commerce Department's Bureau of Industry and Security
added Singapore-based Megaspeed International to the US trade blacklist
for allegedly diverting restricted Nvidia H100 and Blackwell GPUs.

II. US Economy Powers On
While the stock market remains preoccupied with the brouhaha
surrounding the AI trade, the US economy continues to power ahead, and
not merely on the strength of the AI investment boom. Consider the
following:
(1) Consumer Spending. Redbook same-store sales rose 10.3% y/y in
the week ended November 20, well above their 2025 average of 5.8%
(chart). Consumers don't seem to be reining in their spending heading into
the holiday season.

### Page 6

(2) Labor Market. One reason consumer spending should remain strong
is the improving labor market. The Conference Board's November
consumer confidence survey showed that the percentage of consumers
reporting that jobs are plentiful jumped to 30.8%, while the percentage
reporting that jobs are hard to get fell to 18.4%, both their most favorable
readings since early 2025 (chart).
Meanwhile, ADP reported that private-sector employment increased by an

average of 21,000 per week over the four weeks ended November 7, well
above the 16,000 average since 2025 (chart).

### Page 7

(3) Business activity. S&P Global's flash November M-PMI and NM-PMI
eased to 54.5 and 54.7, respectively, but remained comfortably in
expansionary territory (chart). Encouragingly, growth remained broad-
based across both sectors, with hiring activity pointing to another strong
payroll gain in November. Meanwhile, S&P Global's Chris Williamson
noted that the survey's price gauges remain consistent with inflation
running well above the Fed's 2%.
(4) Professor Copper. Then again, the plunge in the copper price in

recent days suggests that the red metal (with a PhD in economics) is
signaling a slower AI buildout, which could slow the economy (chart). The
dollar's recent strength could do the same.

### Page 8

clause.
Yardeni QuickTakes © 2026 – Unsubscribe

## Document 361: Metals sell-off runs ahead of physical markets

Source PDF: all report pdf/Metals sell-off runs ahead of physical markets.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-20
Pages: 6

### Page 1

It has been a diﬃcult week for metals. Copper is down more
than 8%, aluminium almost 6% and gold around 9% as a
stronger dollar, higher rate expectations and weaker risk
appetite weigh on the complex
Ewa Manthey
Metals have come under sharp pressure this week, despite continued tightness
in physical markets
The speed of the sell-oﬀ looks more like a sharp repricing of investor
exposure than a broad weakening in physical markets. Copper has
taken the biggest hit as investors unwind bullish positions and

reassess AI-related demand. Yet the sharp correction in prices
contrasts with still-tight physical markets.
Investors cut bullish metals positions

Investor selling weighs on copper
Copper is on track for one of its steepest weekly declines in a
decade.
We think the speed of the sell-oﬀ partly reﬂects an unwind of bullish
investor positioning. Both LME and COMEX net length have fallen
sharply from recent highs, while positioning across the wider
metals complex has also become less bullish.

Copper has also become more closely correlated with equity
markets, including AI-related stocks. The AI link matters for investor
sentiment, but it does not make copper's demand story dependent
on data centres alone. Grid investment and electriﬁcation remain
important sources of demand. But as investors cut exposure to AI-
related assets, copper has become more vulnerable to the same
selling pressure.

has increased

That contrasts sharply with the physical market in China. Shanghai
inventories are at their lowest since late 2023, while the Yangshan
premium reached a record $500/t this week.
Bloomberg reports that some US copper is now being oﬀered to
buyers in China, where physical availability remains tight. Volumes
are limited so far, but the shift raises the possibility that some of

the metal accumulated in the US ahead of potential tariﬀs could
start returning to the global market.

Copper could remain under pressure if funds continue to cut
positions or the macro backdrop deteriorates. But the price
correction has moved much faster than physical conditions. Tight
availability in China should provide some support once the investor
selling eases.
The risk is that more US metal returns to the wider market if tariﬀ
expectations fade further, gradually easing some of the regional
tightness now supporting the physical market.
Aluminium supply remains tight for now

Aluminium has fallen with the wider complex, but nearby supply
remains tight. The LME cash-to-three-month spread has moved
into a steep backwardation, while LME stocks remain historically
low and largely of Russian origin, limiting what is readily available
to some buyers. This should help contain the near-term downside.
At the same time, investors have reduced bullish aluminium
positions from recent highs, in line with the broader move across
metals.

The medium-term picture is less supportive. Middle Eastern
production is recovering, and new Indonesian capacity is coming
online. We expect the market to move towards surplus next year,
which should limit the scope for a sustained price recovery.
Aluminium spreads point to nearby
tightness

Source: LME, ING Research
Gold feels the pressure from higher rates
Gold has fallen for ﬁve straight sessions and is down around 9%

this week, putting it on course for its steepest weekly decline since
March. Prices have also touched their lowest level since September
2025.
The shift in the macro backdrop has weighed on gold, with higher
rate expectations and a stronger dollar reducing its appeal. The
equity sell-oﬀ may also have prompted some investors to sell gold
to raise cash.
We expect gold to remain vulnerable while rate expectations and
the dollar remain elevated. Central bank demand remains an

important longer-term support, with China adding 26 tonnes to its
reserves in October. But for now, macro forces are likely to set the
direction.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
• US markets were closed for the Thanksgiving public holiday. 
European equities rebounded on Thursday following two 
consecutive sessions of losses. The Euro Stoxx 50 rose 
2.0%, while the FTSE 100 gained 1.8%, its strongest 
advance in more than three weeks.
• Asian equities closed higher following a late recovery on 
Wall Street on Wednesday, though gains were pared after 
reports that Nvidia had told AI hardware manufacturers to 
halt server shipments to Southeast Asia. The Nikkei 225 
gained 0.9%, while the Kospi rose 0.3%, supported by a 
significant upgrade to the Bank of Korea’s 2027 growth 
outlook, as the central bank delivered on its widely expected 
25bp rate hike. The Hang Seng outperformed with a 1.2% 
gain, while the CSI 300 was little changed, up 0.1%. The 
ASX 200 rose 0.8%, led by gains in mining and energy 
stocks, after fresh US tariff proposals spared Australia.
• European yields were broadly steady, although moves were 
more pronounced at the UK front end, with the 2-year gilt 
yield rising 7bps compared with a 2bp increase in the 10-
year, while the 10-year Bund yield was also up 2bps.
• ACGB yields were broadly unchanged, with the 3-year down 
1bp at 4.48% and the 10-year steady at 4.82%. Stronger-
than-expected Q3 capex data did little to shift RBA pricing, 
with the first rate cut still priced by August.
• Currency moves were modest. The USD was little changed, 
with the DXY gaining 0.1% to 103.77. AUD/USD was also 
broadly steady, up 0.1% at 0.6634. Elsewhere, EUR/USD 
was unchanged at 1.0972, while USD/JPY rose 0.1% to 
159.26. The AUD gained 0.4% against the NZD.
• In oil markets, Houthi rebels attacked Saudi Arabia’s Jazan 
refinery and Red Sea ports, with the Saudi-led coalition 
subsequently announcing it had destroyed the ballistic 
missile launch platform used in the attack. OPEC+ is 
reportedly considering extending current output quotas 
into January amid uncertainty surrounding the Iran war, 
ahead of an online meeting on Sunday. Brent futures rose 
2.1% to US$96.6/bbl. Copper rebounded 1.9% from an 
11-month low, supported by a stabilisation in the AI trade, 
while aluminium gained 2.1%. Gold futures fell 0.6% to 
US$3,570/oz, approaching a 15-month low as markets 
continued to price a more hawkish Fed outlook. 
International Data
Spain’s Pablo Hernandez de Cos has been announced as 
Christine Lagarde’s successor as ECB President after a summit 
in Brussels. The European Parliament and ECB Governing 
Council will formally give their support for the decision in 
coming months. Pablo Hernandez de Cos previously served on 
the ECB’s Governing Council while head of Spain’s central bank 
between 2018 and 2024.

October’s ECB meeting minutes, where the stance of policy 
was maintained, showed members largely agreed the inflation 
outlook had deteriorated, and that “further tightening was likely 
necessary” unless the inflation outlook improved significantly.
Domestic Data
Private capex surprised to the upside, rising 12.2%qtr in 
the September quarter of 2026. Following a sizeable 1.6ppt 
upward revision to the June quarter result, annual growth 
accelerated to 18.6%yr. This rise was primarily driven by 
strength in machinery and equipment, which posted its 
strongest quarterly increase on record, up 23.1%qtr and 
34.3%yr (see here).

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2.2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%). 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.5% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect further 25bp increases at the three upcoming meetings (December 2026, January and 
March 2027), bringing the Fed Funds target range at 4.50% - 4.75%.

CHINA 
Economic growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y in Q3 2026 (1.2% q/q) after 4.3% y/y in Q2 and 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a 
whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth remains characterised by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports 
and the fragility of sectors that rely on domestic demand continues to widen. Retail sales barely increased in the first ten months of 2026, the 
crisis in the property sector continues and household confidence remains low. Yet new fiscal policy measures could help support investment 
and private consumption in the very short term. Deflationary pressures are persisting, given weakness in domestic demand. Energy prices and 
electronic goods prices are increasing, producer price inflation is accelerating (5.7% y/y in October), but consumer price inflation remains low 
(0.7% y/y).

EUROZONE 
The economy has shown resilience in the face of the energy shock: GDP grew by 0.4% q/q in Q3, and the 1.0% growth carry-over for 2026 
supports our forecast of 1.1% for the year as a whole (compared to 1.3% in 2025) with the risk tilted to the upside. Growth is expected to 
strengthen to 1.6% in 2027, bolstered by increased spending on defence and infrastructure, as well as the global investment cycle in AI. Annual 
average inflation is thus expected to reach 3.1% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), and 3.2% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, 
we anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause. That 
said, stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q in Q3 2026 (following stagnation in Q2), weighed down by a decline in aeronautics production (delay in the 
delivery of inputs) but supported by service sector output and purchases of electric vehicles. Overall, growth is expected to slow to 0.3% in 
2026 (after 0.9% in 2025) before rebounding to 0.8% in 2027. In parallel, inflation should reach 2.5% in 2026 (compared with 0.9% in 2025), 
before edging down slightly in 2027 (2.4%).

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would accelerate to 1.6% in 2026 (1.2% in 2025). The economy has so far weathered both the energy price shock and domestic 
political volatility, boosted by domestic demand, notably investment. After a series of strong prints from Q1 to Q3, real GDP growth is expected 
to decelerate in the last quarter of the year, as higher energy prices should feed inflation. It should then slow to 1.3% in 2027 on the back of 
higher inflation and more restrictive monetary conditions. Inflation should climb to 3.8% in Q4 2026, peaking in Q1 2027 (at 4.4%) before 
gradually slowing. Still, it is set to remain stubbornly above target, leading to mounting second-round effects risks. In response, the BoE has 
finally raised its Bank Rate to 4.00% early November. If a second-rate hike looks likely in February, this is not, for now, our call and our baseline 
scenario projects that diminishing inflation will allow monetary easing to resume in the second half of 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank 
Rate down to 3.50% by year-end.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 0.9% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.0% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of 2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the US dollar. The dollar's structural downtrend is unlikely to 
resume until 2027, potentially reaching 1.20 by year-end 2027, although political and fiscal risks could weigh on the Euro next year. The 
USD/JPY appreciation is expected to continue, albeit at a more moderate pace, as the BoJ remains behind the curve despite its rate hikes. 
Conversely, GBP/USD would benefit from relative support, bolstered by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government and 
resilient growth.
